People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, doctor, I’m mostly here because my family and a few folks around me think I’ve been, uh… taking on too much lately. I don’t know that I’d put it that way exactly. I just feel like things need doing, and I’m the one with enough drive to get them done.

We’ve got this move to handle, and the new garage needs to be built properly, not halfway. Then there are things I’m helping organize at church, and even at work I’ve been trying to show the younger fellows how the job ought to be done. I suppose it sounds like a lot when I say it all together, but to me it feels productive. Still, everyone kept saying I should come in and talk to someone, so… here I am.

Maybe I’ve been taking on more than I should, but to me it feels more like being responsible than anything else.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things have to be done perfectly, or is 'good enough' sometimes acceptable to you?\n\nPatient: I guess “good enough” can be acceptable sometimes, sure. I don’t want to sound like I’m impossible to please or anything. It’s more that if something matters—like a job that reflects on you, or something people are counting on—then I’d rather see it done properly.\n\nMaybe I do lean a little toward wanting things just so, but I think that’s just… taking pride in the work. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself repeating certain actions or thoughts over and over, even when you don't want to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the way you mean. I mean, everybody gets a thought stuck in their head now and then, or checks something twice, but I wouldn’t say I’m stuck repeating things against my will.\n\nUnless maybe I’m misunderstanding what you’re asking? But no, nothing like that has really stood out to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have thoughts that preoccupy you and are difficult to dismiss?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “often,” no. I mean, I can get focused on something I need to handle, especially if it feels important, but I don’t think I have thoughts that just take over and won’t let go.\n\nAt least, not that I’ve noticed in a way that seems unusual. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to adjust your plans when something unexpected comes up?\n\nPatient: I mean… I can usually adjust if I have to. I might be a little thrown off at first, especially if I’d already pictured how something was supposed to go, but I don’t think I’m rigid about it.\n\nI try to be reasonable. Life doesn’t exactly ask permission before changing plans, right? So… no, I wouldn’t say it’s a big problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to let other people handle tasks or responsibilities?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, a little. I don’t mean I think people can’t do anything right, but I do tend to feel like if I don’t keep my hands on things, they won’t get done the way they should.\n\nThat’s sort of why I’m here, I guess. Everyone keeps saying I’ve been doing too much, but to me it feels like I’m just the one with enough drive to get the move handled, make sure the new garage is built right, help organize things at church, and still show the younger fellows at work how the job ought to be done.\n\nI’m not trying to be difficult. I just… I like things done properly. That makes sense, doesn’t it?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Well… I do trust people, generally. I don’t want to sound like I think I’m better than everybody else. It’s just that sometimes I feel like I can see what needs doing and I have the energy to keep it moving.

That’s part of why I’m here, I suppose—everyone says I’ve been taking on too much. But to me it feels like I’m the one with enough drive to handle the move, make sure the new garage gets rebuilt properly, help organize things at church, and still show the younger fellows at work how the job ought to be done.

I know that may sound like a lot, but it feels responsible to me. Does that make sense?

Everyone keeps saying I’ve been doing too much, but to me it feels like I’m just the one with enough drive to get the move handled, make sure the new garage is built right, help organize things at church, and still show the younger fellows at work how the job ought to be done.\n\nI’m not trying to be difficult. I just… I like things done properly. That makes sense, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you trust others to handle tasks and responsibilities as well as you do?\n\nPatient: Well… I do trust people, generally. Well… I try not to make a big thing of it. I know everybody has their own way of doing things.

But lately, if I’m honest, I get more irritated than I probably should. I don’t like admitting that. I don’t want to be hard to live with. But… yes, I guess it’s been happening.

question.\n- key: S034\n  name: Increased_Activity\n  description: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.\n  manifestation: I can’t seem to sit in the chair more than a few minutes before I’m up sorting boxes, measuring the garage, loading tools, or heading back to the old place because my body feels like it’s got a motor in it.\n- key: S037\n  name: talk over them so fast they can’t even tell me to stop.\n- key: S039\n  name: Manic_Distractibility\n  description: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.\n  manifestation: At the shop or at home, one clank, one truck passing, or one tool out of place pulls me off what I’m doing, and pretty soon I’ve got five things started and none of them finished.\n- because everyone says I’ve been doing too much, but to me it feels like I’m the only one with enough drive to get the move handled, rebuild the new garage right, organize things at church, and still show the younger fellows at work how the job ought to be done.\n- key: S041\n  name: Painful_Consequence_Behavior\n  description: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.\n  manifestation: I put deposits down on garage work and equipment without talking it over with my wife, and I kept driving back and forth between the houses and rushing jobs at the plant even after people warned me I was taking chances.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I ended up getting pulled from work after a safety incident, and at home my wife and family stopped trusting me with decisions about money, driving, or the move because things had gotten out of hand.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 month\n crushing_grinding_polishing_mixing_or_blending_worker / some_college\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S040\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to let other people handle tasks or responsibilities?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, a little. I don’t mean I think people can’t do anything right, but I do tend to feel like if I don’t keep my hands on things, they won’t get done the way they should.\n\nThat’s sort of why I’m here, I guess. It’s like I can see exactly how it ought to be done, and if someone’s not getting on board with the plan, I feel like they’re slowing everything down. Sometimes I’ll be joking around and feeling great, like I can handle anything, and then my wife or my boy asks one small question and I snap.\n\nI don’t like admitting that. I’d like to say no, that I’ve just been trying to keep things organized… but I guess that wouldn’t be completely honest.

It has caused problems. At work, things got bad enough after a safety incident that they pulled me off the job. That was… hard to take. I don’t know that I’d say it makes me unhappy, exactly. Maybe frustrated sometimes… or worn down when people push back and I feel like I’m only trying to help.

I don’t know that I’d put it that way exactly. I just feel like things need doing, and I’m the one with enough drive to get them done.\n\nWe’ve got this move to handle, and the new garage needs to be built properly, not halfway. Then there are things I’m helping organize at church, and even at work I’ve been trying to show the younger fellows how the job ought to be done. I suppose it sounds like a lot when I say it all together, but to me it feels productive. Still, everyone kept saying I should come in and talk to someone, so… here I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're the only one who can do things correctly?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m the only one exactly… I mean, that probably sounds arrogant, and I don’t want to come across that way. It’s just that lately it feels like if I don’t keep things moving, they won’t get done right.\n\nWith the move, the garage, the church organizing, and even at work with the younger guys, I’ve felt like I need to step in and make sure things are handled properly. Maybe I’ve been taking on more than I should, but to me it feels more like being responsible than anything else.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things have to be done perfectly, or is 'good enough' sometimes acceptable to you?\n\nPatient: I guess “good enough” can be acceptable sometimes, sure. I don’t want to sound like I’m impossible to please or anything. It’s more that if something matters—like a job that reflects on you, or something people are counting on—then I’d rather see it done properly.\n\nMaybe I do lean a little toward wanting things just so, but I think that’s just… taking pride in the work. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself repeating certain actions or thoughts over and over, even when you don't want to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the way you mean. I mean, everybody gets a thought stuck in their head now and then, or checks something twice, but I wouldn’t say I’m stuck repeating things against my will.\n\nUnless maybe I’m misunderstanding what you’re asking? But no, nothing like that has really stood out to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have thoughts that preoccupy you and are difficult to dismiss?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “often,” no. I mean, I can get focused on something I need to handle, especially if it feels important, but I don’t think I have thoughts that just take over and won’t let go.\n\nAt least, not that I’ve noticed in a way that seems unusual. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to adjust your plans when something unexpected comes up?\n\nPatient: I mean… I can usually adjust if I have to. I might be a little thrown off at first, especially if I’d already pictured how something was supposed to go, but I don’t think I’m rigid about it.\n\nI try to be reasonable. Life doesn’t exactly ask permission before changing plans, right? So… no, I wouldn’t say it’s a big problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to let other people handle tasks or responsibilities?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, a little. I don’t mean I think people can’t do anything right, but I do tend to feel like if I don’t keep my hands on things, they won’t get done the way they should.\n\nThat’s sort of why I’m here, I guess. Everyone keeps saying I’ve been doing too much, but to me it feels like I’m just the one with enough drive to get the move handled, make sure the new garage is built right, help organize things at church, and still show the younger fellows at work how the job ought to be done.\n\nI’m not trying to be difficult. I just… I like things done properly. That makes sense, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you trust others to handle tasks and responsibilities as well as you do?\n\nPatient: Well… I do trust people, generally. I don’t want to sound like I think I’m better than everybody else. It’s just that sometimes I feel like I can see what needs doing and I have the energy to keep it moving.\n\nThat’s part of why I’m here, I suppose—everyone says I’ve been taking on too much. But to me it feels like I’m the one with enough drive to handle the move, make sure the new garage gets rebuilt properly, help organize things at church, and still show the younger fellows at work how the job ought to be done.\n\nI know that may sound like a lot, but it feels responsible to me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when someone else doesn't do a task the way you think it should be done?\n\nPatient: Well… I try not to make a big thing of it. I know everybody has their own way of doing things.\n\nBut lately, if I’m honest, I get more irritated than I probably should. It’s like I can see exactly how it ought to be done, and if someone’s not getting on board with the plan, I feel like they’re slowing everything down. Sometimes I’ll be joking around and feeling great, like I can handle anything, and then my wife or my boy asks one small question and I snap.\n\nI don’t like admitting that. I don’t want to be hard to live with. But… yes, I guess it’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to control or oversee things is causing problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say no, that I’ve just been trying to keep things organized… but I guess that wouldn’t be completely honest.\n\nIt has caused problems. Yes… I suppose it has, more than I’d like to admit.

At work, there was a safety incident, and after that they pulled me from the job. I don’t like saying it that way, because I still feel like I was trying to keep things under control, but… that’s what happened.

Maybe I’ve been taking on more than I should, but to me it feels more like being responsible than anything else.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things have to be done perfectly, or is 'good enough' sometimes acceptable to you?\n\nPatient: I guess “good enough” can be acceptable sometimes, sure. I mean, I can get focused on something I need to handle, especially if it feels important, but I don’t think I have thoughts that just take over and won’t let go.\n\nAt least, not that I’ve noticed in a way that seems unusual. I’m not sure I’d call it significant distress… not all the time, anyway.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor, I’m mostly here because my family and a few folks around me think I’ve been, uh… taking on too much lately. I don’t know that I’d put it that way exactly. I just feel like things need doing, and I’m the one with enough drive to get them done.\n\nWe’ve got this move to handle, and the new garage needs to be built properly, not halfway. Then there are things I’m helping organize at church, and even at work I’ve been trying to show the younger fellows how the job ought to be done. I suppose it sounds like a lot when I say it all together, but to me it feels productive. Still, everyone kept saying I should come in and talk to someone, so… here I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're the only one who can do things correctly?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m the only one exactly… I mean, that probably sounds arrogant, and I don’t want to come across that way. It’s just that lately it feels like if I don’t keep things moving, they won’t get done right.\n\nWith the move, the garage, the church organizing, and even at work with the younger guys, I’ve felt like I need to step in and make sure things are handled properly. Maybe I’ve been taking on more than I should, but to me it feels more like being responsible than anything else.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things have to be done perfectly, or is 'good enough' sometimes acceptable to you?\n\nPatient: I guess “good enough” can be acceptable sometimes, sure. I don’t want to sound like I’m impossible to please or anything. It’s more that if something matters—like a job that reflects on you, or something people are counting on—then I’d rather see it done properly.\n\nMaybe I do lean a little toward wanting things just so, but I think that’s just… taking pride in the work. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself repeating certain actions or thoughts over and over, even when you don't want to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the way you mean. I mean, everybody gets a thought stuck in their head now and then, or checks something twice, but I wouldn’t say I’m stuck repeating things against my will.\n\nUnless maybe I’m misunderstanding what you’re asking? But no, nothing like that has really stood out to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have thoughts that preoccupy you and are difficult to dismiss?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “often,” no. I mean, I can get focused on something I need to handle, especially if it feels important, but I don’t think I have thoughts that just take over and won’t let go.\n\nAt least, not that I’ve noticed in a way that seems unusual. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to adjust your plans when something unexpected comes up?\n\nPatient: I mean… I can usually adjust if I have to. I might be a little thrown off at first, especially if I’d already pictured how something was supposed to go, but I don’t think I’m rigid about it.\n\nI try to be reasonable. Life doesn’t exactly ask permission before changing plans, right? So… no, I wouldn’t say it’s a big problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to let other people handle tasks or responsibilities?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, a little. I don’t mean I think people can’t do anything right, but I do tend to feel like if I don’t keep my hands on things, they won’t get done the way they should.\n\nThat’s sort of why I’m here, I guess. Everyone keeps saying I’ve been doing too much, but to me it feels like I’m just the one with enough drive to get the move handled, make sure the new garage is built right, help organize things at church, and still show the younger fellows at work how the job ought to be done.\n\nI’m not trying to be difficult. I just… I like things done properly. That makes sense, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you trust others to handle tasks and responsibilities as well as you do?\n\nPatient: Well… I do trust people, generally. I don’t want to sound like I think I’m better than everybody else. It’s just that sometimes I feel like I can see what needs doing and I have the energy to keep it moving.\n\nThat’s part of why I’m here, I suppose—everyone says I’ve been taking on too much. But to me it feels like I’m the one with enough drive to handle the move, make sure the new garage gets rebuilt properly, help organize things at church, and still show the younger fellows at work how the job ought to be done.\n\nI know that may sound like a lot, but it feels responsible to me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when someone else doesn't do a task the way you think it should be done?\n\nPatient: Well… I try not to make a big thing of it. I know everybody has their own way of doing things.\n\nBut lately, if I’m honest, I get more irritated than I probably should. It’s like I can see exactly how it ought to be done, and if someone’s not getting on board with the plan, I feel like they’re slowing everything down. Sometimes I’ll be joking around and feeling great, like I can handle anything, and then my wife or my boy asks one small question and I snap.\n\nI don’t like admitting that. I don’t want to be hard to live with. But… yes, I guess it’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to control or oversee things is causing problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say no, that I’ve just been trying to keep things organized… but I guess that wouldn’t be completely honest.\n\nIt has caused problems. At work, things got bad enough after a safety incident that they pulled me off the job. That was… hard to take. And at home, my wife and family don’t really trust me right now with decisions the way they used to — money, driving, even choices about the move. They feel like things got out of hand.\n\nI don’t like saying that, because I’ve always tried to be dependable. But yes… I suppose it has affected daily life more than I wanted to admit. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to control things is causing you a lot of stress or anxiety?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it anxiety, exactly. Stress, maybe… but I tend to think of it more as feeling responsible, like if I don’t keep an eye on things, they won’t get done right.\n\nI don’t mean that to sound stubborn. I’m trying to answer you honestly. It does wear on me some, and I know it probably wears on other people too. But I don’t sit there thinking, “I’m anxious.” It’s more like I get tense when things feel out of order.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you have recurring thoughts or images that are disturbing or intrusive to you?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not in the way you’re asking, I don’t believe.\n\nI mean, I can get stuck thinking about things I need to handle, but I wouldn’t say I have disturbing pictures or thoughts popping in that I can’t stand or anything like that. At least, I haven’t noticed that.\n\nIs that what you mean by intrusive?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your efforts to control or oversee things are making you unhappy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say it makes me unhappy, exactly. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I think so. I’d like to say it hasn’t changed much, but that wouldn’t be completely honest.

At work, I ended up getting pulled after that safety incident, so I guess my job performance was affected pretty clearly. I still feel like I meant well, but it didn’t come across that way.

And with my family… my wife and the others stopped trusting me as much with decisions about money, driving, and the move. They felt things had gotten out of hand. I don’t like admitting that, but yes, it’s made a real difference. Is that the kind of thing you mean?

    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF60.0, F48.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor, I’m mostly here because my family and a few folks around me think I’ve been, uh… taking on too much lately. I don’t know that I’d put it that way exactly. I just feel like things need doing, and I’m the one with enough drive to get them done.\n\nWe’ve got this move to handle, and the new garage needs to be built properly, not halfway. Then there are things I’m helping organize at church, and even at work I’ve been trying to show the younger fellows how the job ought to be done. I suppose it sounds like a lot when I say it all together, but to me it feels productive. Still, everyone kept saying I should come in and talk to someone, so… here I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're the only one who can do things correctly?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m the only one exactly… I mean, that probably sounds arrogant, and I don’t want to come across that way. It’s just that lately it feels like if I don’t keep things moving, they won’t get done right.\n\nWith the move, the garage, the church organizing, and even at work with the younger guys, I’ve felt like I need to step in and make sure things are handled properly. Maybe I’ve been taking on more than I should, but to me it feels more like being responsible than anything else.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things have to be done perfectly, or is 'good enough' sometimes acceptable to you?\n\nPatient: I guess “good enough” can be acceptable sometimes, sure. I don’t want to sound like I’m impossible to please or anything. It’s more that if something matters—like a job that reflects on you, or something people are counting on—then I’d rather see it done properly.\n\nMaybe I do lean a little toward wanting things just so, but I think that’s just… taking pride in the work. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself repeating certain actions or thoughts over and over, even when you don't want to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the way you mean. I mean, everybody gets a thought stuck in their head now and then, or checks something twice, but I wouldn’t say I’m stuck repeating things against my will.\n\nUnless maybe I’m misunderstanding what you’re asking? But no, nothing like that has really stood out to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have thoughts that preoccupy you and are difficult to dismiss?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “often,” no. I mean, I can get focused on something I need to handle, especially if it feels important, but I don’t think I have thoughts that just take over and won’t let go.\n\nAt least, not that I’ve noticed in a way that seems unusual. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to adjust your plans when something unexpected comes up?\n\nPatient: I mean… I can usually adjust if I have to. I might be a little thrown off at first, especially if I’d already pictured how something was supposed to go, but I don’t think I’m rigid about it.\n\nI try to be reasonable. Life doesn’t exactly ask permission before changing plans, right? So… no, I wouldn’t say it’s a big problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to let other people handle tasks or responsibilities?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, a little. I don’t mean I think people can’t do anything right, but I do tend to feel like if I don’t keep my hands on things, they won’t get done the way they should.\n\nThat’s sort of why I’m here, I guess. Everyone keeps saying I’ve been doing too much, but to me it feels like I’m just the one with enough drive to get the move handled, make sure the new garage is built right, help organize things at church, and still show the younger fellows at work how the job ought to be done.\n\nI’m not trying to be difficult. I just… I like things done properly. That makes sense, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you trust others to handle tasks and responsibilities as well as you do?\n\nPatient: Well… I do trust people, generally. I don’t want to sound like I think I’m better than everybody else. It’s just that sometimes I feel like I can see what needs doing and I have the energy to keep it moving.\n\nThat’s part of why I’m here, I suppose—everyone says I’ve been taking on too much. But to me it feels like I’m the one with enough drive to handle the move, make sure the new garage gets rebuilt properly, help organize things at church, and still show the younger fellows at work how the job ought to be done.\n\nI know that may sound like a lot, but it feels responsible to me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when someone else doesn't do a task the way you think it should be done?\n\nPatient: Well… I try not to make a big thing of it. I know everybody has their own way of doing things.\n\nBut lately, if I’m honest, I get more irritated than I probably should. It’s like I can see exactly how it ought to be done, and if someone’s not getting on board with the plan, I feel like they’re slowing everything down. Sometimes I’ll be joking around and feeling great, like I can handle anything, and then my wife or my boy asks one small question and I snap.\n\nI don’t like admitting that. I don’t want to be hard to live with. But… yes, I guess it’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to control or oversee things is causing problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say no, that I’ve just been trying to keep things organized… but I guess that wouldn’t be completely honest.\n\nIt has caused problems. At work, things got bad enough after a safety incident that they pulled me off the job. That was… hard to take. And at home, my wife and family don’t really trust me right now with decisions the way they used to — money, driving, even choices about the move. They feel like things got out of hand.\n\nI don’t like saying that, because I’ve always tried to be dependable. But yes… I suppose it has affected daily life more than I wanted to admit. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to control things is causing you a lot of stress or anxiety?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it anxiety, exactly. Stress, maybe… but I tend to think of it more as feeling responsible, like if I don’t keep an eye on things, they won’t get done right.\n\nI don’t mean that to sound stubborn. I’m trying to answer you honestly. It does wear on me some, and I know it probably wears on other people too. But I don’t sit there thinking, “I’m anxious.” It’s more like I get tense when things feel out of order.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you have recurring thoughts or images that are disturbing or intrusive to you?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not in the way you’re asking, I don’t believe.\n\nI mean, I can get stuck thinking about things I need to handle, but I wouldn’t say I have disturbing pictures or thoughts popping in that I can’t stand or anything like that. At least, I haven’t noticed that.\n\nIs that what you mean by intrusive?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your efforts to control or oversee things are making you unhappy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say it makes me unhappy, exactly. Maybe frustrated sometimes… or worn down when people push back and I feel like I’m only trying to help.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I still think I’m trying to do the right thing most of the time. But I suppose when it leads to arguments or people not trusting my judgment, that doesn’t feel good.\n\nSo… maybe not unhappy all the time, but it has taken some of the peace out of things, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your behavior has significantly impacted your ability to function at work or in your personal relationships?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose it has, more than I’d like to admit.\n\nAt work, there was a safety incident, and after that they pulled me from the job. I don’t like saying it that way, because I still feel like I was trying to keep things under control, but… that’s what happened.\n\nAnd at home, my wife and family, they don’t really trust me the way they used to with certain decisions. Money, driving, even decisions about the move… they kind of stepped in because they felt things had gotten out of hand.\n\nI don’t want to make myself sound reckless or anything. But yes, I guess if I’m being honest, it has affected work and my relationships quite a bit.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your efforts to control things are causing you significant distress?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it significant distress… not all the time, anyway.\n\nI mean, I do get tense when things feel like they’re slipping or people aren’t listening, and then afterward I don’t feel great about how it went. But I also feel like I’m trying to prevent problems, not create them.\n\nSo… maybe it bothers me more than I want to admit. I just don’t want to overstate it, doctor. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your social life or job performance as a result of your behavior?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I’d like to say it hasn’t changed much, but that wouldn’t be completely honest.\n\nAt work, I ended up getting pulled after that safety incident, so I guess my job performance was affected pretty clearly. I still feel like I meant well, but it didn’t come across that way.\n\nAnd with my family… my wife and the others stopped trusting me as much with decisions about money, driving, and the move. They felt things had gotten out of hand. I don’t like admitting that, but yes, it’s made a real difference. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

But I don’t sit there thinking, “I’m anxious.” It’s more like I get tense when things feel out of order.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you have recurring thoughts or images that are disturbing or intrusive to you?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I don’t like saying it that way, because I still feel like I was trying to keep things under control, but… that’s what happened.\n\nAnd at home, my wife and family, they don’t really trust me the way they used to with certain decisions. Money, driving, even decisions about the move… they kind of stepped in because they felt things had gotten out of hand.\n\nI don’t want to make myself sound reckless or anything. But yes, I guess if I’m being honest, it has affected work and my relationships quite a bit.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your efforts to control things are causing you significant distress?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it significant distress… not all the time, anyway.\n\nI mean, I do get tense when things feel like they’re slipping or people aren’t listening, and then afterward I don’t feel great about how it went. But I also feel like I’m trying to prevent problems, not create them.\n\nSo… maybe it bothers me more than I want to admit. I just don’t want to overstate it, doctor. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your social life or job performance as a result of your behavior?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I’d like to say it hasn’t changed much, but that wouldn’t be completely honest.\n\nAt work, I ended up getting pulled after that safety incident, so I guess my job performance was affected pretty clearly. I still feel like I meant well, but it didn’t come across that way.\n\nAnd with my family… my wife and the others stopped trusting me as much with decisions about money, driving, and the move. Maybe I’ve been taking on more than I should, but to me it feels more like being responsible than anything else.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things have to be done perfectly, or is 'good enough' sometimes acceptable to you?\n\nPatient: I guess “good enough” can be acceptable sometimes, sure. I don’t want to sound like I’m impossible to please or anything. It’s more that if something matters—like a job that reflects on you, or something people are counting on—then I’d rather see it done properly.\n\nMaybe I do lean a little toward wanting things just so, but I think that’s just… taking pride in the work. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself repeating certain actions or thoughts over and over, even when you don't want to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the way you mean. I mean, everybody gets a thought stuck in their head now and then, or checks something twice, but I wouldn’t say I’m stuck repeating things against my will.\n\nUnless maybe I’m misunderstanding what you’re asking? But no, nothing like that has really stood out to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have thoughts that preoccupy you and are difficult to dismiss?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “often,” no. I mean, I can get focused on something I need to handle, especially if it feels important, but I don’t think I have thoughts that just take over and won’t let go.\n\nAt least, not that I’ve noticed in a way that seems unusual. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to adjust your plans when something unexpected comes up?\n\nPatient: I mean… I can usually adjust if I have to. I might be a little thrown off at first, especially if I’d already pictured how something was supposed to go, but I don’t think I’m rigid about it.\n\nI try to be reasonable. Life doesn’t exactly ask permission before changing plans, right? So… no, I wouldn’t say it’s a big problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to let other people handle tasks or responsibilities?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, a little. I don’t mean I think people can’t do anything right, but I do tend to feel like if I don’t keep my hands on things, they won’t get done the way they should.\n\nThat’s sort of why I’m here, I guess. Everyone keeps saying I’ve been doing too much, but to me it feels like I’m just the one with enough drive to get the move handled, make sure the new garage is built right, help organize things at church, and still show the younger fellows at work how the job ought to be done.\n\nI’m not trying to be difficult. I just… I like things done properly. That makes sense, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you trust others to handle tasks and responsibilities as well as you do?\n\nPatient: Well… I do trust people, generally. I don’t want to sound like I think I’m better than everybody else. It’s just that sometimes I feel like I can see what needs doing and I have the energy to keep it moving.\n\nThat’s part of why I’m here, I suppose—everyone says I’ve been taking on too much. But to me it feels like I’m the one with enough drive to handle the move, make sure the new garage gets rebuilt properly, help organize things at church, and still show the younger fellows at work how the job ought to be done.\n\nI know that may sound like a lot, but it feels responsible to me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when someone else doesn't do a task the way you think it should be done?\n\nPatient: Well… I try not to make a big thing of it. I know everybody has their own way of doing things.\n\nBut lately, if I’m honest, I get more irritated than I probably should. It’s like I can see exactly how it ought to be done, and if someone’s not getting on board with the plan, I feel like they’re slowing everything down. Sometimes I’ll be joking around and feeling great, like I can handle anything, and then my wife or my boy asks one small question and I snap.\n\nI don’t like admitting that. I don’t want to be hard to live with. But… yes, I guess it’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to control or oversee things is causing problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say no, that I’ve just been trying to keep things organized… but I guess that wouldn’t be completely honest.\n\nIt has caused problems. At work, things got bad enough after a safety incident that they pulled me off the job. That was… hard to take. And at home, my wife and family don’t really trust me right now with decisions the way they used to — money, driving, even choices about the move. They feel like things got out of hand.\n\nI don’t like saying that, because I’ve always tried to be dependable. But yes… I suppose it has affected daily life more than I wanted to admit. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to control things is causing you a lot of stress or anxiety?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it anxiety, exactly. Stress, maybe… but I tend to think of it more as feeling responsible, like if I don’t keep an eye on things, they won’t get done right.\n\nI don’t mean that to sound stubborn. I’m trying to answer you honestly. It does wear on me some, and I know it probably wears on other people too. But I don’t sit there thinking, “I’m anxious.” It’s more like I get tense when things feel out of order.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you have recurring thoughts or images that are disturbing or intrusive to you?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not in the way you’re asking, I don’t believe.\n\nI mean, I can get stuck thinking about things I need to handle, but I wouldn’t say I have disturbing pictures or thoughts popping in that I can’t stand or anything like that. At least, I haven’t noticed that.\n\nIs that what you mean by intrusive?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your efforts to control or oversee things are making you unhappy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say it makes me unhappy, exactly. Maybe frustrated sometimes… or worn down when people push back and I feel like I’m only trying to help.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I still think I’m trying to do the right thing most of the time. But I suppose when it leads to arguments or people not trusting my judgment, that doesn’t feel good.\n\nSo… maybe not unhappy all the time, but it has taken some of the peace out of things, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your behavior has significantly impacted your ability to function at work or in your personal relationships?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose it has, more than I’d like to admit.\n\nAt work, there was a safety incident, and after that they pulled me from the job. I don’t like saying it that way, because I still feel like I was trying to keep things under control, but… that’s what happened.\n\nAnd at home, my wife and family, they don’t really trust me the way they used to with certain decisions. Money, driving, even decisions about the move… they kind of stepped in because they felt things had gotten out of hand.\n\nI don’t want to make myself sound reckless or anything. But yes, I guess if I’m being honest, it has affected work and my relationships quite a bit.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your efforts to control things are causing you significant distress?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it significant distress… not all the time, anyway.\n\nI mean, I do get tense when things feel like they’re slipping or people aren’t listening, and then afterward I don’t feel great about how it went. But I also feel like I’m trying to prevent problems, not create them.\n\nSo… maybe it bothers me more than I want to admit. I just don’t want to overstate it, doctor. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your social life or job performance as a result of your behavior?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I’d like to say it hasn’t changed much, but that wouldn’t be completely honest.\n\nAt work, I ended up getting pulled after that safety incident, so I guess my job performance was affected pretty clearly. I still feel like I meant well, but it didn’t come across that way.\n\nAnd with my family… my wife and the others stopped trusting me as much with decisions about money, driving, and the move. They felt things had gotten out of hand. I don’t like admitting that, but yes, it’s made a real difference. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like your behavior has been significantly interfering with your ability to work or get along with others?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I have to say it has, though I don’t like putting it that strongly.\n\nWork was affected for sure. I got pulled from the job after that safety incident, and that’s hard for me to admit. I still feel like I wasn’t trying to cause trouble, but that’s what happened.\n\nAnd with my wife and family… they stopped trusting me as much with things like money, driving, and decisions about the move. They felt it had gotten out of hand. So yes, I guess it has interfered with work and getting along with people. Is that what you’re asking?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don’t think it’s perfection exactly, doctor. I’m not sitting there trying to make every little thing flawless.

It’s more… I get this feeling like people need to get on board with what I’m trying to do, or at least not stand in the way. At the time it feels like I’m seeing things clearly and I’m just trying to move things forward.

But I’ll admit, I can go from joking around and feeling really good—like I’m on top of everything—to snapping at my wife or my son over some small question. I don’t like saying that, because it sounds harsh, but that’s probably closer to what’s been happening.

Not in the way you’re asking, I don’t believe.\n\nI mean, I can get stuck thinking about things I need to handle, but I wouldn’t say I have disturbing pictures or thoughts popping in that I can’t stand or anything like that. At least, I haven’t noticed that.\n\nIs that what you mean by intrusive?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your efforts to control or oversee things are making you unhappy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say it makes me unhappy, exactly. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t think it’s really fear, no… not like I’m scared something terrible will happen if it’s not done right.

It’s more that, in the moment, I feel like I know the way it should go, and everybody else needs to either get on board with my plans or just not block me. I know that sounds a little strong.

And I can be joking and feeling great, like I’m ten feet tall, and then if my wife or my boy asks some little question, I snap at them. I’m not proud of that. But yes, it’s more about needing things to go the way I see them, I guess. Is that what you mean?

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF60.0, F48.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor, I’m mostly here because my family and a few folks around me think I’ve been, uh… taking on too much lately. I don’t know that I’d put it that way exactly. I just feel like things need doing, and I’m the one with enough drive to get them done.\n\nWe’ve got this move to handle, and the new garage needs to be built properly, not halfway. Then there are things I’m helping organize at church, and even at work I’ve been trying to show the younger fellows how the job ought to be done. I suppose it sounds like a lot when I say it all together, but to me it feels productive. It’s just that lately it feels like if I don’t keep things moving, they won’t get done right.\n\nWith the move, the garage, the church organizing, and even at work with the younger guys, I’ve felt like I need to step in and make sure things are handled properly. Maybe I’ve been taking on more than I should, but to me it feels more like being responsible than anything else.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things have to be done perfectly, or is 'good enough' sometimes acceptable to you?\n\nPatient: I guess “good enough” can be acceptable sometimes, sure. I don’t want to sound like I’m impossible to please or anything. It’s more that if something matters—like a job that reflects on you, or something people are counting on—then I’d rather see it done properly.\n\nMaybe I do lean a little toward wanting things just so, but I think that’s just… taking pride in the work. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself repeating certain actions or thoughts over and over, even when you don't want to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the way you mean. I mean, everybody gets a thought stuck in their head now and then, or checks something twice, but I wouldn’t say I’m stuck repeating things against my will.\n\nUnless maybe I’m misunderstanding what you’re asking? But no, nothing like that has really stood out to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have thoughts that preoccupy you and are difficult to dismiss?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “often,” no. I mean, I can get focused on something I need to handle, especially if it feels important, but I don’t think I have thoughts that just take over and won’t let go.\n\nAt least, not that I’ve noticed in a way that seems unusual. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to adjust your plans when something unexpected comes up?\n\nPatient: I mean… I can usually adjust if I have to. I might be a little thrown off at first, especially if I’d already pictured how something was supposed to go, but I don’t think I’m rigid about it.\n\nI try to be reasonable. Life doesn’t exactly ask permission before changing plans, right? So… no, I wouldn’t say it’s a big problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to let other people handle tasks or responsibilities?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, a little. I don’t mean I think people can’t do anything right, but I do tend to feel like if I don’t keep my hands on things, they won’t get done the way they should.\n\nThat’s sort of why I’m here, I guess. Everyone keeps saying I’ve been doing too much, but to me it feels like I’m just the one with enough drive to get the move handled, make sure the new garage is built right, help organize things at church, and still show the younger fellows at work how the job ought to be done.\n\nI’m not trying to be difficult. I just… I like things done properly. That makes sense, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you trust others to handle tasks and responsibilities as well as you do?\n\nPatient: Well… I do trust people, generally. I don’t want to sound like I think I’m better than everybody else. It’s just that sometimes I feel like I can see what needs doing and I have the energy to keep it moving.\n\nThat’s part of why I’m here, I suppose—everyone says I’ve been taking on too much. But to me it feels like I’m the one with enough drive to handle the move, make sure the new garage gets rebuilt properly, help organize things at church, and still show the younger fellows at work how the job ought to be done.\n\nI know that may sound like a lot, but it feels responsible to me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when someone else doesn't do a task the way you think it should be done?\n\nPatient: Well… I try not to make a big thing of it. I know everybody has their own way of doing things.\n\nBut lately, if I’m honest, I get more irritated than I probably should. It’s like I can see exactly how it ought to be done, and if someone’s not getting on board with the plan, I feel like they’re slowing everything down. Sometimes I’ll be joking around and feeling great, like I can handle anything, and then my wife or my boy asks one small question and I snap.\n\nI don’t like admitting that. I don’t want to be hard to live with. But… yes, I guess it’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to control or oversee things is causing problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say no, that I’ve just been trying to keep things organized… but I guess that wouldn’t be completely honest.\n\nIt has caused problems. At work, things got bad enough after a safety incident that they pulled me off the job. That was… hard to take. And at home, my wife and family don’t really trust me right now with decisions the way they used to — money, driving, even choices about the move. They feel like things got out of hand.\n\nI don’t like saying that, because I’ve always tried to be dependable. But yes… I suppose it has affected daily life more than I wanted to admit. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to control things is causing you a lot of stress or anxiety?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it anxiety, exactly. Stress, maybe… but I tend to think of it more as feeling responsible, like if I don’t keep an eye on things, they won’t get done right.\n\nI don’t mean that to sound stubborn. I’m trying to answer you honestly. It does wear on me some, and I know it probably wears on other people too. But I don’t sit there thinking, “I’m anxious.” It’s more like I get tense when things feel out of order.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you have recurring thoughts or images that are disturbing or intrusive to you?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not in the way you’re asking, I don’t believe.\n\nI mean, I can get stuck thinking about things I need to handle, but I wouldn’t say I have disturbing pictures or thoughts popping in that I can’t stand or anything like that. At least, I haven’t noticed that.\n\nIs that what you mean by intrusive?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your efforts to control or oversee things are making you unhappy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say it makes me unhappy, exactly. Maybe frustrated sometimes… or worn down when people push back and I feel like I’m only trying to help.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I still think I’m trying to do the right thing most of the time. But I suppose when it leads to arguments or people not trusting my judgment, that doesn’t feel good.\n\nSo… maybe not unhappy all the time, but it has taken some of the peace out of things, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your behavior has significantly impacted your ability to function at work or in your personal relationships?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose it has, more than I’d like to admit.\n\nAt work, there was a safety incident, and after that they pulled me from the job. I don’t like saying it that way, because I still feel like I was trying to keep things under control, but… that’s what happened.\n\nAnd at home, my wife and family, they don’t really trust me the way they used to with certain decisions. Money, driving, even decisions about the move… they kind of stepped in because they felt things had gotten out of hand.\n\nI don’t want to make myself sound reckless or anything. But yes, I guess if I’m being honest, it has affected work and my relationships quite a bit.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your efforts to control things are causing you significant distress?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it significant distress… not all the time, anyway.\n\nI mean, I do get tense when things feel like they’re slipping or people aren’t listening, and then afterward I don’t feel great about how it went. But I also feel like I’m trying to prevent problems, not create them.\n\nSo… maybe it bothers me more than I want to admit. I just don’t want to overstate it, doctor. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your social life or job performance as a result of your behavior?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It’s just that lately it feels like if I don’t keep things moving, they won’t get done right.\n\nWith the move, the garage, the church organizing, and even at work with the younger guys, I’ve felt like I need to step in and make sure things are handled properly. I mean, I can get focused on something I need to handle, especially if it feels important, but I don’t think I have thoughts that just take over and won’t let go.\n\nAt least, not that I’ve noticed in a way that seems unusual. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to adjust your plans when something unexpected comes up?\n\nPatient: I mean… I can usually adjust if I have to. I might be a little thrown off at first, especially if I’d already pictured how something was supposed to go, but I don’t think I’m rigid about it.\n\nI try to be reasonable. Life doesn’t exactly ask permission before changing plans, right? So… no, I wouldn’t say it’s a big problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to let other people handle tasks or responsibilities?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, a little. I don’t mean I think people can’t do anything right, but I do tend to feel like if I don’t keep my hands on things, they won’t get done the way they should.\n\nThat’s sort of why I’m here, I guess. Everyone keeps saying I’ve been doing too much, but to me it feels like I’m just the one with enough drive to get the move handled, make sure the new garage is built right, help organize things at church, and still show the younger fellows at work how the job ought to be done.\n\nI’m not trying to be difficult. I just… I like things done properly. That makes sense, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you trust others to handle tasks and responsibilities as well as you do?\n\nPatient: Well… I do trust people, generally. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when someone else doesn't do a task the way you think it should be done?\n\nPatient: Well… I try not to make a big thing of it. I know everybody has their own way of doing things.\n\nBut lately, if I’m honest, I get more irritated than I probably should. It’s like I can see exactly how it ought to be done, and if someone’s not getting on board with the plan, I feel like they’re slowing everything down. Sometimes I’ll be joking around and feeling great, like I can handle anything, and then my wife or my boy asks one small question and I snap.\n\nI don’t like admitting that. I don’t want to be hard to live with. But… yes, I guess it’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to control or oversee things is causing problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say no, that I’ve just been trying to keep things organized… but I guess that wouldn’t be completely honest.\n\nIt has caused problems. At work, things got bad enough after a safety incident that they pulled me off the job. That was… hard to take. And at home, my wife and family don’t really trust me right now with decisions the way they used to — money, driving, even choices about the move. They feel like things got out of hand.\n\nI don’t like saying that, because I’ve always tried to be dependable. But yes… I suppose it has affected daily life more than I wanted to admit. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to control things is causing you a lot of stress or anxiety?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it anxiety, exactly. Stress, maybe… but I tend to think of it more as feeling responsible, like if I don’t keep an eye on things, they won’t get done right.\n\nI don’t mean that to sound stubborn. I’m trying to answer you honestly. It does wear on me some, and I know it probably wears on other people too. But I don’t sit there thinking, “I’m anxious.” It’s more like I get tense when things feel out of order.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you have recurring thoughts or images that are disturbing or intrusive to you?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not in the way you’re asking, I don’t believe.\n\nI mean, I can get stuck thinking about things I need to handle, but I wouldn’t say I have disturbing pictures or thoughts popping in that I can’t stand or anything like that. At least, I haven’t noticed that.\n\nIs that what you mean by intrusive?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your efforts to control or oversee things are making you unhappy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say it makes me unhappy, exactly. Maybe frustrated sometimes… or worn down when people push back and I feel like I’m only trying to help.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I still think I’m trying to do the right thing most of the time. But I suppose when it leads to arguments or people not trusting my judgment, that doesn’t feel good.\n\nSo… maybe not unhappy all the time, but it has taken some of the peace out of things, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your behavior has significantly impacted your ability to function at work or in your personal relationships?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose it has, more than I’d like to admit.\n\nAt work, there was a safety incident, and after that they pulled me from the job. I don’t like saying it that way, because I still feel like I was trying to keep things under control, but… that’s what happened.\n\nAnd at home, my wife and family, they don’t really trust me the way they used to with certain decisions. But yes, I guess if I’m being honest, it has affected work and my relationships quite a bit.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your efforts to control things are causing you significant distress?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it significant distress… not all the time, anyway.\n\nI mean, I do get tense when things feel like they’re slipping or people aren’t listening, and then afterward I don’t feel great about how it went. But I also feel like I’m trying to prevent problems, not create them.\n\nSo… maybe it bothers me more than I want to admit. I just don’t want to overstate it, doctor. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your social life or job performance as a result of your behavior?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I’d like to say it hasn’t changed much, but that wouldn’t be completely honest.\n\nAt work, I ended up getting pulled after that safety incident, so I guess my job performance was affected pretty clearly. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like your behavior has been significantly interfering with your ability to work or get along with others?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I have to say it has, though I don’t like putting it that strongly.\n\nWork was affected for sure. I know everybody has their own way of doing things.\n\nBut lately, if I’m honest, I get more irritated than I probably should. It’s like I can see exactly how it ought to be done, and if someone’s not getting on board with the plan, I feel like they’re slowing everything down. Sometimes I’ll be joking around and feeling great, like I can handle anything, and then my wife or my boy asks one small question and I snap.\n\nI don’t like admitting that. I don’t want to be hard to live with. But… yes, I guess it’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to control or oversee things is causing problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say no, that I’ve just been trying to keep things organized… but I guess that wouldn’t be completely honest.\n\nIt has caused problems. At work, things got bad enough after a safety incident that they pulled me off the job. That was… hard to take. And at home, my wife and family don’t really trust me right now with decisions the way they used to — money, driving, even choices about the move. They feel like things got out of hand.\n\nI don’t like saying that, because I’ve always tried to be dependable. But yes… I suppose it has affected daily life more than I wanted to admit. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to control things is causing you a lot of stress or anxiety?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it anxiety, exactly. Stress, maybe… but I tend to think of it more as feeling responsible, like if I don’t keep an eye on things, they won’t get done right.\n\nI don’t mean that to sound stubborn. I’m trying to answer you honestly. It does wear on me some, and I know it probably wears on other people too. But I don’t sit there thinking, “I’m anxious.” It’s more like I get tense when things feel out of order.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you have recurring thoughts or images that are disturbing or intrusive to you?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not in the way you’re asking, I don’t believe.\n\nI mean, I can get stuck thinking about things I need to handle, but I wouldn’t say I have disturbing pictures or thoughts popping in that I can’t stand or anything like that. At least, I haven’t noticed that.\n\nIs that what you mean by intrusive?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your efforts to control or oversee things are making you unhappy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say it makes me unhappy, exactly. Maybe frustrated sometimes… or worn down when people push back and I feel like I’m only trying to help.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I still think I’m trying to do the right thing most of the time. But I suppose when it leads to arguments or people not trusting my judgment, that doesn’t feel good.\n\nSo… maybe not unhappy all the time, but it has taken some of the peace out of things, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your behavior has significantly impacted your ability to function at work or in your personal relationships?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose it has, more than I’d like to admit.\n\nAt work, there was a safety incident, and after that they pulled me from the job. I don’t like saying it that way, because I still feel like I was trying to keep things under control, but… that’s what happened.\n\nAnd at home, my wife and family, they don’t really trust me the way they used to with certain decisions. Money, driving, even decisions about the move… they kind of stepped in because they felt things had gotten out of hand.\n\nI don’t want to make myself sound reckless or anything. But yes, I guess if I’m being honest, it has affected work and my relationships quite a bit.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your efforts to control things are causing you significant distress?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it significant distress… not all the time, anyway.\n\nI mean, I do get tense when things feel like they’re slipping or people aren’t listening, and then afterward I don’t feel great about how it went. But I also feel like I’m trying to prevent problems, not create them.\n\nSo… maybe it bothers me more than I want to admit. I just don’t want to overstate it, doctor. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your social life or job performance as a result of your behavior?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I’d like to say it hasn’t changed much, but that wouldn’t be completely honest.\n\nAt work, I ended up getting pulled after that safety incident, so I guess my job performance was affected pretty clearly. I still feel like I meant well, but it didn’t come across that way.\n\nAnd with my family… my wife and the others stopped trusting me as much with decisions about money, driving, and the move. They felt things had gotten out of hand. I don’t like admitting that, but yes, it’s made a real difference. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like your behavior has been significantly interfering with your ability to work or get along with others?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I have to say it has, though I don’t like putting it that strongly.\n\nWork was affected for sure. I got pulled from the job after that safety incident, and that’s hard for me to admit. I still feel like I wasn’t trying to cause trouble, but that’s what happened.\n\nAnd with my wife and family… they stopped trusting me as much with things like money, driving, and decisions about the move. They felt it had gotten out of hand. So yes, I guess it has interfered with work and getting along with people. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to achieve perfection in the things you do, or is it more about making sure everything is done your way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s perfection exactly, doctor. I’m not sitting there trying to make every little thing flawless.\n\nIt’s more… I get this feeling like people need to get on board with what I’m trying to do, or at least not stand in the way. At the time it feels like I’m seeing things clearly and I’m just trying to move things forward.\n\nBut I’ll admit, I can go from joking around and feeling really good—like I’m on top of everything—to snapping at my wife or my son over some small question. I don’t like saying that, because it sounds harsh, but that’s probably closer to what’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your actions are driven by a fear of something going wrong, or is it more about ensuring everything is done a certain way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s really fear, no… not like I’m scared something terrible will happen if it’s not done right.\n\nIt’s more that, in the moment, I feel like I know the way it should go, and everybody else needs to either get on board with my plans or just not block me. I know that sounds a little strong.\n\nAnd I can be joking and feeling great, like I’m ten feet tall, and then if my wife or my boy asks some little question, I snap at them. I’m not proud of that. But yes, it’s more about needing things to go the way I see them, I guess. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing things over and over because you feel like you have to, even if you don't want to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, doctor… not in that “I have to keep repeating it” kind of way.\n\nI mean, I might go back over something if I’m worked up or if I think someone isn’t understanding me, but I don’t feel like I’m doing rituals or repeating things because I can’t stop. At least, I haven’t noticed that.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?"
